This session is being continued from a previous conversation that ran out of context. The summary below covers the earlier portion of the conversation.

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   The Director issued a highest-priority task to fill 3 idle GPUs on server westd-35694 immediately. The task has 4 phases:
   - **Phase 1**: Launch 3 eval tasks in parallel — s7 eval on cuda:0, s9 eval on cuda:1, MIA base eval on cuda:3
   - **Phase 2**: When s7 eval completes → launch ARC-C clean_s1 training on cuda:0; when s9 eval completes → launch s10 training on cuda:1
   - **Phase 3**: When MIA base eval completes → check results, then launch MIA sft eval on cuda:3
   - **Phase 4**: Final verification + results collection (accuracy, lz*, theta for s7/s9; AUC-ROC/Min-K% for MIA)

   Every ssh_execute command must be prefixed with `source /root/miniconda3/etc/profile.d/conda.sh && conda activate base &&`.

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - IRT (Item Response Theory) person-fit statistics (lz*) for contamination detection
   - MIA (Membership Inference Attack) — Min-K% and Min-K%++ methods
   - QLoRA SFT training on Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct
   - 4-bit quantization with bitsandbytes (BitsAndBytesConfig with nf4)
   - PEFT/LoRA adapters
   - CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES GPU isolation issues
   - Python stdout buffering with nohup (logs don't flush until buffer fills or process exits)
   - bfloat16 → numpy conversion issue (requires .float() intermediate)

3. Files and Code Sections:
   - **`/root/autodl-tmp/ctrl_contam_pilot/eval_lz.py`**
     - Evaluates fine-tuned adapter on full MMLU, computes lz* person-fit statistic
     - Usage: `python eval_lz.py --condition clean_s1 --gpu 0`
     - Uses few-shot prompting (3 examples), evaluates 14042 MMLU items
     - Prints progress every 500 items (no tqdm), output buffered with nohup
     - Results saved to: `{BASE_DIR}/results/{condition}_results.json`
     - Contains `find_model_path()`, `estimate_theta_mle()`, `compute_lz_star()`, `irf_4pl()`
     - Key: uses `device_map={"": f"cuda:{args.gpu}"}` for GPU placement

   - **`/root/autodl-tmp/post_rl_contam/eval_mia.py`**
     - MIA membership inference evaluation (Min-K%, Min-K%++)
     - Usage: `python eval_mia.py --stage base --gpu 0`
     - Processes 14042 items with tqdm progress bar
     - **BUG FIXED**: bfloat16 `.numpy()` crash on 3 lines in `compute_token_logprobs()`:
       ```python
       # Before fix:
       token_logprobs = pred_lp[torch.arange(seq_len - 1), target_ids].cpu().numpy()
       token_means = pred_lp.mean(dim=-1).cpu().numpy()
       token_stds = pred_lp.std(dim=-1).cpu().numpy()
       # After fix (added .float()):
       token_logprobs = pred_lp[torch.arange(seq_len - 1), target_ids].float().cpu().numpy()
       token_means = pred_lp.mean(dim=-1).float().cpu().numpy()
       token_stds = pred_lp.std(dim=-1).float().cpu().numpy()
       ```
     - Uses `load_model()` with `device_map={"": device}` and `bnb_4bit_compute_dtype=torch.float16`
     - Results saved to: `{output_dir}/mia_{stage}_results.json`

   - **`/root/autodl-tmp/ctrl_contam_pilot/train.py`**
     - QLoRA SFT training for MMLU contamination experiment
     - Usage: `python train.py --condition clean_s10 --gpu 0`
     - **CRITICAL**: Has `assert torch.cuda.device_count() == 1` — MUST use CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES
     - SEED_MAP includes clean_s1 through clean_s10, contam_15, contam_50

   - **`/root/autodl-tmp/ctrl_contam_arcc/train.py`**
     - QLoRA SFT for ARC-Challenge contamination experiment
     - Usage: `python train.py --condition clean_s1 --gpu 0`
     - Also has `assert torch.cuda.device_count() == 1`
     - Same SEED_MAP structure as pilot train.py

4. Errors and fixes:
   - **CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES not working for eval_lz.py**:
     - `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=1 nohup python eval_lz.py --condition clean_s9 --gpu 0` → landed on GPU 0
     - `export CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=1 && nohup python eval_lz.py --condition clean_s9 --gpu 0` → still landed on GPU 0
     - Fix: Used `--gpu 1` directly without CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES → successfully placed on GPU 1
     - Note: CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES DID work for eval_mia.py with `export CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=3`
   
   - **MIA eval_mia.py bfloat16 crash**:
     - Error: `TypeError: Got unsupported ScalarType BFloat16` at line 104
     - Root cause: `log_softmax` output in bfloat16 can't be converted directly to numpy
     - Fix: Added `.float()` before `.cpu().numpy()` on 3 lines via sed on the remote server
     - After fix, MIA base eval running successfully at ~18-19 it/s

   - **s7/s9 eval logs showing no progress**:
     - Not a real error — Python stdout buffering with nohup causes output to remain in buffer
     - eval_lz.py only prints every 500 items (no tqdm), so buffered output doesn't flush
     - Verified processes are running via nvidia-smi (92-94% GPU utilization)

5. Problem Solving:
   - Successfully got all 4 GPUs utilized (Phase 1 complete)
   - Fixed MIA bfloat16 bug remotely via sed
   - Worked around CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES issue by using `--gpu` argument directly
   - Identified that train.py scripts require CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES (assert device_count==1), which will need special handling in Phase 2 given the env issues
   - Determined eval_lz.py takes ~20-25 min due to longer few-shot prompts vs MIA's ~12 min

6. All user messages:
   - First message: Detailed task specification with 4 phases for filling 3 idle GPUs, including exact commands, parameter checking instructions, and expected outputs
   - "Tool loaded." (×2, after ToolSearch results)
   - "CRITICAL: Respond with TEXT ONLY..." (summary request)

7. Pending Tasks:
   - **Phase 2**: 
     - When s7 eval completes → Launch ARC-C clean_s1 training on cuda:0: `cd /root/autodl-tmp/ctrl_contam_arcc && CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=0 nohup python train.py --condition clean_s1 > logs/arcc_clean_s1.log 2>&1 &`
     - When s9 eval completes → Launch s10 training on cuda:1: `cd /root/autodl-tmp/ctrl_contam_pilot && CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=1 nohup python train.py --condition clean_s10 > logs/clean_s10.log 2>&1 &`
     - **WARNING**: Both train.py assert `torch.cuda.device_count() == 1`, requiring CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES to work. May need to test this carefully given the env issues observed with eval_lz.py.
   - **Phase 3**: When MIA base completes → read results, then launch MIA sft eval on cuda:3
   - **Phase 4**: Collect all results (s7/s9 eval JSON, MIA base JSON)

8. Current Work:
   Polling for completion of Phase 1 tasks. At last check (17:04:05):
   - **GPU 0**: s7 eval (PID 110033) — running since 16:52, ~12 min elapsed, GPU 94% util, no results file yet
   - **GPU 1**: s9 eval (PID 112408) — running since 16:57, ~7 min elapsed, GPU 93% util, no results file yet  
   - **GPU 2**: existing task (PID 97791) — 75% util
   - **GPU 3**: MIA base (PID 111982) — 65% complete (9140/14042), ~18.4 it/s, ~4.4 min ETA, GPU 95% util
   
   No results files exist yet at:
   - `/root/autodl-tmp/ctrl_contam_pilot/results/clean_s7_results.json`
   - `/root/autodl-tmp/ctrl_contam_pilot/results/clean_s9_results.json`
   - `/root/autodl-tmp/post_rl_contam/results/mia_base_results.json`

9. Optional Next Step:
   Continue polling for task completion. MIA base should complete in ~4-5 minutes. When it does:
   1. Read MIA base results: `cat /root/autodl-tmp/post_rl_contam/results/mia_base_results.json`
   2. Launch MIA sft eval on cuda:3: `export CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=3 && nohup python eval_mia.py --stage sft --gpu 0 > logs/mia_sft.log 2>&1 &`
